class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"232\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"234\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x047c01","title":"The Widow Takes the Oars","volume":"婚后外传：公爵输给了一位寡妇","anchor":"chapter-0235","paragraphs":["The club's oldest widow challenged Cassian to a rowing race. Her late husband had been a celebrated oarsman. Cassian had been a celebrated owner of expensive shoes. Mara accepted the challenge for him before he could identify the flaw in this comparison.","The riverside club had invited friends to celebrate its centenary. A Swiss boatbuilder adjusted a stiff rowlock while a workman checked the landing. A shepherd had brought wool blankets, a shipper had lent a van, and a shopkeeper had supplied fruit. The ever-changing river required preparations that made the prevailing wish for a simple outing rather optimistic.","A salesman had donated waterproof bags. Mara tried one and found its strap too stiff. Cassian offered to tackle it, then tried to hide a rip with his thumb. He had been a user for less than a minute. She promised not to make the bag a scapegoat for his strength. Their small accident did not diminish the spirit of the morning.","A reviewer from a travel paper wanted a romantic photograph. The club spokesperson directed him to the Victorian boathouse. Its moist walls needed repair, but the photographer could find a good angle. The stylist preparing the group portrait wanted everyone to face the light. An optical reflection from the windows kept defeating her. Behind the scenes, the waitress found a better place for the tea table and solved three problems at once.","The club chairwoman was a retired civil servant. Its guest of honour was a former speaker pro tempore, now a devoted walker. He had once been a statesman and preferred being called a pensioner. An accountant and an analyst were trying to reconcile the entry list. Their systematicness impressed Mara until both counted the same dog as a member.","A worshipper of old boats wanted to seek out a neglected shell in the shed. He was a regular walker along the southern riverbank and had seen the club grow. He worked productively when given a brush. The chairwoman would regularly find him cleaning a rail nobody had noticed was dirty. To name but a few useful volunteers, he and the tea makers made the club possible.","The dog belonged to a scout leader and had accompanied a teenager to training for years. The teenager's uncle, a veteran sailor, wanted him to compete with the adults. A sober-sided scholar asked whether the criteria were fair. The club would not segregate beginners merely to flatter experienced rowers. The banker's team could lose without anyone losing individual worth.","The undergraduates had chosen the film Terminator for a later screening. A sophomore made the robot voice while a typist printed tickets. A vegetarian usher checked the snack table. The donor who had proposed an antipoverty project wanted to speak first. Mara suggested he keep it brief. A performative display of concern could consume the whole evening if nobody watched the clock.","Cassian had become a sightseer rather than a contender. He admired the youth team's boat until the widow handed him an oar. The stylist tried to fix his hair. He asked her not to give away his total lack of experience before the race. His unfortunate opponent laughed, then taught him how to hold the handle. She had decided he was worthy of instruction.","A shareholder in the local ferry had volunteered as a steward. An archaeologist was helping a museum affiliate prepare a river-history table. The local conductor brought a string quartet. A bachelor banker, an enthusiastic citizen and a former convict all signed up to help unload chairs. The last had worked with the club's reintegration programme and wanted no public speech about his past.","Mara liked that arrangement. An achiever could be proud of a quiet task. A public arbiter of everyone's worth was unnecessary. The retired speaker pro tempore agreed. They would carry out the practical plan, however successful or otherwise the photographs became. The future weather was unknowable; the forecast was merely useful.","A civilian from the nearby rescue association checked the float line. An alarmist wanted to terminate the race before it began. The chairwoman found his forecast too vague. A worthwhile precaution could be taken without declaring every unknown current unknowable doom. Her persuasive reply gave the man something to do: keep watching the weather and report a definite change.","Nessa, a twenty-four-year-old waitress, brought the widow tea. Her fiancé Felix, a scholar of local history, carried the tray too carefully. He was hoping to give her a ring upgrade during the evening. She wanted him to stop buying things and rehearse their wedding dance. Mara, who overheard, offered a place on the terrace after the race.","A burglar alarm sounded in the empty changing room. Its claimant was a spider moving across a sensor. The flawed signal did not make the spider a criminal, the caretaker announced. Felix laughed, the first relaxed sound he had made all day. Mara thought digital technology had given him a useful gift for once.","A swift shadow crossed the water. A bird dipped near the landing. Then a motorboat's wake struck the floating platform, and the front pair of chairs slid towards the edge. The dog barked. The second hit his knees and pushed him backwards into the river. Mara's alarm lasted until his head appeared. The water was shallow beside the landing, and the safety steward had already thrown a float. Her husband emerged wet, indignant and still holding a perfectly usable chair.","The widow called him worthy of a medal. He asked whether one could be supplied dry. A workman helped him onto the platform. The Swiss boatbuilder checked the supports, while the shipper fetched a towel from the van. The shepherd's blanket acquired an aristocratic customer. Mara wrapped it around Cassian and forgot to hide her relief.","The shepherd reclaimed his blanket only to add a second. A scholar of old sports records tried to compensate for the wait with a story. The waitress handed Cassian tea. Their attention to one wet soul was generous, though he would have preferred to sneak into a dry room without an audience. Mara guided him there herself.","The club's spokesperson stopped the photographer. A person in trouble was not free entertainment. The reviewer put his camera away. The club would compensate for damaged equipment, but it would not buy a more dramatic account of the fall. A regular sightseer at the gate offered a statement about the passing boat. His view would be useful whether he was a supporter or otherwise.","A stakeholder from the boatbuilding firm checked the platform. Its load had been distributed unevenly, and a moist plank had become slippery. They could make a repair now. He asked people to keep moving along the safe side. The club would not keep blaming the passing wake for a problem it could fix itself.","The scout leader and teenager checked the lifejackets before the race resumed. A veteran sailor examined the landing with the boy's uncle. The club had no desire to segregate the youth team, but experience mattered for certain jobs. The scholar's sober-sided warning had been right. The prevailing wish to keep the programme moving needed a safe boundary.","Behind the scenes, Nessa found dry clothing. Felix had brought a spare shirt for the evening. It fit Cassian poorly and made the stylist despair. The wardrobe emergency seemed to tackle all her plans at once. Her generosity nevertheless prevailed. She adjusted a cuff, gave Mara a comb and abandoned the portrait's original uniformity.","The salesman had a spare bag for the wet shirt. Cassian promised not to rip this one. The man's refusal to give away his amusement was heroic. Mara thought him a worshipper of discretion. The ever-changing look on her husband's face was worth the price of a fresh shirt. He threatened to terminate her delight by kissing her.","The sophomore rehearsed the Terminator voice until the typist threatened to confiscate his tickets. Husband","volume":"婚后外传：公爵输给了一位寡妇","anchor":"chapter-0237","paragraphs":["The widow won by three boat lengths. Cassian blamed the borrowed shirt. She said it was a fine shirt and offered him a second lesson. He accepted with a bow. Mara applauded the generosity of both parties, then stole the fruit prize before he could claim it as emotional compensation.","The club's retired speaker pro tempore returned the microphone. His final speech was one sentence: tea was ready. Its systematicness could not be faulted. The guests moved indoors in an orderly stream, regularly pausing to let wet competitors pass. The day had been worthwhile even for the losers, though the widow saw no reason to dwell on that category.","The kitchen became the centre of the celebration. Nessa worked productively beside the chef while Felix rehearsed his private speech outside. Mara found him pacing near the hedge. He wanted to offer the ring in a way that would make Nessa happy. She suggested he seek out the person he intended to marry and listen to what she had asked for.","A stakeholder in the club's antipoverty programme had offered free lessons for local youth. The idea was to keep learning affordable through young adulthood. One might become a convert to rowing, another might simply find a companion for weekends. Their outcomes need not be identical to be worthwhile.","He had heard the request for a dance, but treated it as modesty. Mara thought that assumption was risky. Her words were not a riddle with an unknowable answer. He could accept them. He looked down at the expensive box, then put it in his pocket. The decision seemed to make him lighter.","Cassian had borrowed a second shirt, this one less unfortunate. He had already begun charming the widow into a rematch next summer. A supporter of endless optimism, she promised to consider it. Mara had never seen him so willing to be bad at something in public. She found the effect dangerously attractive.","His original shirt hung unevenly over a chair, with a long rip at the cuff. The workman offered to mend it. Cassian refused to make a scapegoat of the landing, but agreed to the repair. A user of expensive clothes could still learn to value a useful patch. Mara liked the lesson more than he did.","The screening equipment was finally ready. A teenager and an undergraduate adjusted the sound while a veteran checked the cables. Their collaboration had little need for a performative speech about unity. Each knew a useful part. They would seek out missing help when they needed it. To name but a few tasks, somebody had to run the projector, wash cups and find the dog.","A stiff walker sat beside the window, grateful for tea after a long day. No prize could compensate for a dry chair at the right moment, he said. Cassian understood that in his soul. Mara tried to sneak him another biscuit before the widow noticed; she was too late.","The dog had joined a shopper outside. Its owner, the scout leader, reclaimed it with apologies. The animal had regularly received extra biscuits throughout the day. Whether that counted as achievement or otherwise was a question the club did not intend to settle. Cassian offered a final bribe and lost the animal's loyalty the moment Mara moved towards the cake.","The day had not been in vain. The club had found a safer way to receive visitors, a volunteer had found a friend and Felix had found a better question than the price of a ring. The individual stories were small. Their sum, to Mara, was the reason they had come in the first place.","On the terrace, Felix asked Nessa to dance. He did not reveal the ring. She stared for one pleased second, then put her hands on his shoulders. Their first steps were dreadful. Her laugh carried across the garden. The mistake cost him nothing and gave her exactly the evening she had wanted.","Mara watched from the doorway. “He listened.”","Cassian slipped his arms around her. “A formidable strategy.”","“You should try it.”","“I have regularly survived by it.”","She turned into his embrace. The hall behind them was bright with music and mismatched clothes. The river outside had become a smooth dark ribbon. Its morning temper had passed, though nobody sensible would assume tomorrow's current was known. They had done enough planning for one day.","He drew her onto the grass. She could feel the damp through her shoes and the warmth of him through the borrowed linen. Their steps were easier than Felix's, but no more serious. A small laugh escaped her when Cassian deliberately turned the wrong way to avoid the photographer.","The picture would have been handsome. She preferred the dance they kept."],"questions":[],"wordCount":793,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0237\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵输给了一位寡妇</div><h1>The Prize Goes to the Wrong Husband</h1><p id=\"chapter-0237-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9232\" data-words=\"9232\">widow</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9243\" data-words=\"9243\">won</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1196\" data-words=\"1196\">by</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8484\" data-words=\"8484\">three</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1023\" data-words=\"1023\">boat</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4806\" data-words=\"4806\">lengths</a>. the last ride on the giant wheel. Cassian wanted to keep the evening simple: dinner, fireworks, home. A designer friend said that was a linear plan and therefore doomed. Mara found the judgment persuasive. Her husband had never survived a fairground without winning something unnecessarily large.","A graduate student stood beside a voluntary ticket collector. The student, a young neuroscientist, was visiting with a physiologist and a dentist. All three looked ready to abandon their professions for candyfloss. A marine on leave carried the bags. His guardian in childhood, now a retiree, asked him to win her a small stuffed fairy. He chose the largest dragon on the stand instead.","Beside an ever-rising pile of prizes, the reigning champion at the ring toss was an eccentric housewife who had already won three lamps. A notable local merchant wanted her secret. Her European neighbour offered to sell him advice. The retailer running the stall watched this threat to his business with amusement. The fair was profit-oriented, but it had room for a little generosity. He gave a youngster an extra throw.","The would-be champion beside her was Cassian. He tried mightily to make the rings obey him. A dozen throws or so produced no prize. The staff member told him not to invert his wrist. He chose to take a page from the elderly winner instead, watching how she relaxed before releasing. All his hopes of dignity might perish, but he would get Mara a toy.","Mara and Cassian moved towards the music pavilion. An instrumentalist played an old love song instrumentally while a small group sang something else over it. The combination was terribly cheerful. Cassian thought a spoken verse might help; Mara suggested he indulge the musicians instead. He could not reorganize every evening in return for being invited.","A park parent hurriedly rescued a naked doll from a puddle. A little player had left it beside a carved emperor's throne in the puppet theatre. The emperor, fairy and revolutionary sailor had been packed away, but a god in a paper cloud remained over the stage. A democratic committee of children demanded one more scene. The secretary gave the puppeteer another five minutes.","A fond grandparent watched the youngsters hunt for a lost crown. Their ability to cooperate had once seemed a valid miracle. Now she regarded it as proof that games could be useful. The park's founder, a scientist with a taste for fireworks, had wanted to make learning employable only in the widest sense: curiosity should help people live, not merely sell themselves.","The current overseer had inherited that project from his predecessor. He wanted to seek to preserve its pleasures while updating its equipment. A special-interest group representing neighbours wanted quieter evenings. A labour rights campaigner wanted shorter late shifts. The balance was difficult, though Mara disliked treating people instrumentally whenever their request complicated the schedule.","The evening's main point was pleasure. To put it another way, a guest should not need a plan for every minute. A belated announcement asked the puppeteer to unplug a dim, faulty lamp. Previously it had flickered without failing. Its repeated warnings had become monotonous, but the technician would not ignore them. The repair was a little unfashionable compared with the dancing lights, and more useful.","The founder's old slogan had called the park a social cure. An antismoking advocate preferred a practical garden with seats. The park could not cure every loneliness, she said, but it could offer a welcoming place to meet. Mara liked the unfashionable modesty of that thought. Not every good idea required a revolutionary name.","A group of young visitors wanted to compile a small playgoing guide. They offered help in the form of a map for nervous first-timers. Its keyword was WELCOME. Every child could add a review individually. Mara thought it optimistic, but not foolish. The guidelines would go into effect next month, after the library checked accessibility.","Cassian bought her a paper hat. She put it on him. The impulsive purchase suited him rather well, though the seller had intended it for a minor. His attempt to look neutral failed when the child beside them began to laugh. The reigning champion awarded him a consolation kiss from his wife. Mara considered the prize entirely valid.","Then a giant balloon broke loose from the puppet stage. It floated into the wheel's lowest spoke. The operator stopped the mechanism. Above them, eight occupied cabins remained suspended. Mara looked up at the quiet structure, then at Cassian. Their reserved cabin was still empty. For once they had been late to exactly the right thing."],"questions":[],"wordCount":773,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0238\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵输给了一只摩天轮</div><h1>The Giant Wheel Refuses a Duke</h1><p id=\"chapter-0238-p1\">Mara&#x27;s <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1968\" data-words=\"1968\">cousin</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3834\" data-words=\"3834\">had</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1041\" data-words=\"1041\">booked</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4727\" data-words=\"4727\">last</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7110\" data-words=\"7110\">ride</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5700\" data-words=\"5700\">on</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3582\" data-words=\"3582\">giant</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9195\" data-words=\"9195\">wheel</a>. Charge","volume":"婚后外传：公爵输给了一只摩天轮","anchor":"chapter-0239","paragraphs":["The wheel operator followed the stopping procedure and called his supervisor. The cabins were secure, he told waiting families. A technician was checking the snag. Mara watched a child wave from above and waved back. There was no need to make fear the evening's main point before they knew the problem.","The reigning supervisor sought to do everything in a sensible order. The ride's inspection team would seek to do the same. A special-interest group had raised labour rights concerns about ever-longer shifts, and fatigue mattered here. The profit-oriented park needed people alert enough to make safe decisions, not too tired to speak.","An electrician arrived with tools. A laborer cleared the area while a surgeon helped an anxious visitor sit. The visitor's spouse had chosen the highest cabin and hated waiting there. His amateur diagnosis of a breakdown grew more dramatic by the minute. The surgeon suggested a chair and water instead of making him an invalid in public.","A passerby offered to climb. The operator refused. His impulsive kindness was appreciated, but another unprotected person at height would not improve the rescue. The current ruler of the control booth was a woman with fifteen years' experience. She needed room to work, not a would-be hero. Cassian recognized the look and helped keep the path clear.","The belated electrician had to unplug a dim light before checking its cable. His ever-rising pile of replaced parts looked unglamorous beside the fairground. Mara admired the unfashionable work. He accepted her thanks, then returned to the lamp.","The giant balloon had not touched the main cable. It had tangled with a decorative bracket, leaving its line wrapped where the wheel should move. The mechanic explained the situation to the supervisor. The public would receive an update in the form of a short announcement, not a stream of technical guesses. Previously, the park had promised instant replies. Tonight it promised useful ones.","The technician worked mightily on an awkward clamp, then changed tools. A straight, linear movement would release the line; he need not invert the whole bracket. His naked forearms were damp with sweat. The public saw twenty minutes or so of waiting. He saw one difficult fastening that had to move without disturbing anything else.","The band moved beneath the pavilion roof. Its leader played the melody instrumentally, slow enough that waiting families could talk. A singer stopped to help an eater whose ice cream had begun dripping over a phone. The smartphone survived; the napkin did not. A fellow graduate supplied another. Their ordinary assistance helped more than a claim that music solved everything.","Mara offered to indulge the waiting children with a story in return for a quieter queue. She would take a page from the puppet theatre and let them choose the ending. The old emperor's fictional kingdom would perish if he kept refusing to listen. To put it another way, even a paper ruler might need advice.","Mara found her cousin hurriedly trying to reassure a dependent uncle. He had planned to propose in the top cabin and was now trapped with the ring in his pocket. His intended bride knew nothing of it. She was a surgeon's old colleague, not easily frightened, but he could imagine only every possible failure. Mara asked him to stop inventing the woman's answer before asking the question.","Nearby, a pilgrim on his way to a coastal shrine sat beside a national fencing player. A former ruler's statue looked down on both. The athlete was an irregular visitor; the pilgrim knew every bench. They agreed the bronze emperor's expression had become silly in the changing light. A neutral observer could perhaps have denied it, but neither was inclined to be neutral.","The dim lamp by the pavilion produced a terribly long shadow. A child said a monster was coming. The pilgrim turned it into a hat with his hands, and the moment passed. The park's slogan about a social cure seemed ambitious, but a kind stranger could still make waiting easier. A neighbor brought chairs from a nearby shop.","A scold near the barrier blamed the people in the cabins for delaying everyone else. Cassian interrupted with perfect calm. The strangers had done nothing wrong. Calling them the culprits would not give the waiting crowd a solution. A neutral dealer in antique maps, attending with his family, supported him. The angry man walked away.","The park planned to compile a review after reopening. A representative wanted to reinforce the new neighbour-contact scheme before it went into effect. The antismoking advocate asked whether there would be benches. Her question was practical and specific. The ever-rising list of ambitions should not swallow a place to sit.","The mechanic freed the balloon. The wheel restarted slowly under supervision, and the first cabin descended. Its occupants looked relieved enough to laugh at the paper crown Cassian still wore. He had forgotten it. Mara made no move to remind him."],"questions":[],"wordCount":824,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0239\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵输给了一只摩天轮</div><h1>The Paper Crown Takes Charge</h1><p id=\"chapter-0239-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9195\" data-words=\"9195\">wheel</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5745\" data-words=\"5745\">operator</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3332\" data-words=\"3332\">followed</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7989\" data-words=\"7989\">stopping</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6480\" data-words=\"6480\">procedure</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1215\" data-words=\"1215\">called</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3941\" data-words=\"3941,3907\">his</a> supervisor. <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1204\" data-words=\"1204\">cabins</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-792\" data-words=\"792\">were</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7346\" data-words=\"7346\">secure</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3847\" data-words=\"3847\">he</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8327\" data-words=\"8327\">told</a> waiting <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3128\" data-words=\"3128\">families</a>. <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">A</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8314\" data-words=\"8314\">technician</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-792\" data-words=\"792\">was</a> checking <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> snag.
